# Candidate policy requirements inventory

Local extraction aid for the first [deployment checklist item](checklist.md). Not approved policy or evidence of an implemented engine.
See [review instructions](README.md#requirements-inventory). Edit only review fields; add missed claims using MANUAL IDs. Absent claims are historical, never applicable requirements.
Numeric HTML entities preserve exact source wording, including newlines and Markdown characters. No generation date is a review date.

## Scanned sources

File hashes identify the scanned source revision, not approval. Changed context still requires human review even when a quoted claim is unchanged.

| C-f6bd652b35a1d60fde44-1-1 | [src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx](../src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx#L93) | 93 | # TRUSTED RESEARCH ENVIRONMENT (TRE) CLINICAL DATA GOVERNANCE CHARTER&#10;*Standard Operating Protocol &#38; Statutory Compliance Specification*&#10;*Document Reference: TRE-PAC-GOV-2026-V4 &#124; Approved by Trust Board &#38; Caldicott Guardian*&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 1. Statutory Mandate &#38; Legal Framework&#10;This Trusted Research Environment (TRE) operates in strict accordance with UK health data legislation:&#10;- **UK General Data Protection Regulation (UK GDPR)**:&#10;  - Article 6(1)(e): Processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 9(2)(j): Processing of special category health data is necessary for archiving and scientific research purposes in the public interest.&#10;- **Data Protection Act 2018 (DPA 2018)**: Part 2 and Section 19 safeguards for scientific research.&#10;- **National Health Service Act 2006 (Section 251)**: Statutory exemption granted on advice of the Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG) permitting processing of patient data without consent for essential medical research.&#10;- **Health and Social Care Act 2012**: Mandatory statutory directions on secure health data custody.&#10;- **Common Law Duty of Confidentiality (CLDC)**: Enforced via the Eight Caldicott Principles.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 2. Institutional Roles &#38; Oversight Structure&#10;- **Caldicott Guardian**: Responsible for championing confidentiality and reviewing all Tier 3 data access requests.&#10;- **Senior Information Risk Owner (SIRO)**: Board-level accountability for technical and cyber risks across the cloud enclave.&#10;- **Data Protection Officer (DPO)**: Conducts Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIA) and audits compliance with UK GDPR.&#10;- **Research Ethics Committee (REC)**: Independent health ethics body reviewing all research questions for demonstrable public benefit.&#10;- **System-Level Security Custodian**: Technical lead maintaining zero-egress VPC controls and Open Policy Agent rule compilation.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 3. The Five Safes Operational Standard&#10;Every project instantiated within the TRE must mathematically satisfy the Five Safes framework:&#10;1. **Safe People**:&#10;   - Mandatory institutional accreditation with verified university or NHS employment.&#10;   - Active, annual NHS Information Governance (IG) training certification.&#10;   - Signed binding Data User Agreements with legal penalties for misuse.&#10;2. **Safe Projects**:&#10;   - Active approval letter from an accredited NHS Research Ethics Committee (REC).&#10;   - Valid Section 251 CAG exemption or explicit patient consent.&#10;   - Documented research protocol demonstrating public health benefit.&#10;3. **Safe Settings**:&#10;   - Isolated Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) with zero external internet routing.&#10;   - Inbound access restricted to multi-factor authenticated (MFA) virtual desktops and notebooks.&#10;   - Prohibition of local disk copying, clipboard exfiltration, or unauthorized external API connections.&#10;4. **Safe Data**:&#10;   - Permanent redaction of direct identifiers (NHS number, patient names, full postcodes, full DOBs).&#10;   - Partitioning into hospital operational tiers (Tier 1 Aggregate, Tier 2 Pseudonymised, Tier 3 Section 251 Sensitive).&#10;   - Minimization: researchers receive only approved study columns and demographic slices.&#10;5. **Safe Outputs**:&#10;   - Compulsory digital airlock review on all summary tables, regression outputs, and machine learning models.&#10;   - Enforced Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) thresholds prior to release.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 4. Quantitative Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) Protocol&#10;All analytical outputs submitted to the airlock must conform to national standards (ONS and SAIL Databank):&#10;- **Primary Cell Suppression ($k &#92;&#92;ge 10$)**:&#10;  - Any frequency count or cell in a summary table representing fewer than 10 individuals ($k &#60; 10$) must be suppressed or grouped.&#10;- **Dominance Rule ($(1, 80&#92;&#92;%)$ or $n,k$ rule)**:&#10;  - If a single patient or clinic accounts for more than 80% of an aggregated metric (e.g. expenditure or bed days), the cell must be masked to prevent attribute disclosure.&#10;- **Secondary (Residual) Suppression**:&#10;  - Additional non-sensitive cells must be suppressed to ensure that suppressed cells cannot be deduced by subtracting remaining cells from row or column totals.&#10;- **Tabular Aggregation Limits**:&#10;  - Maxima, minima, and percentiles (p0, p100) are prohibited; interquartile ranges and median figures must be used.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 5. Artificial Intelligence &#38; Autonomous Analytics Agent Policy&#10;When autonomous LLMs or code assistants operate within the TRE:&#10;- Workloads are classified under the **EU AI Act** (Limited Risk for code synthesis; High Risk for diagnostic inference).&#10;- Agent execution is strictly governed by **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** tool boundaries:&#10;  - &#92;&#96;mcp:ExecuteSQL&#92;&#96;: Barred from executing DDL/DML (DROP, ALTER, UPDATE, INSERT); limited to read-only &#92;&#96;SELECT&#92;&#96; with row caps (max 500 rows).&#10;  - &#92;&#96;mcp:ExportSummary&#92;&#96;: Forced through automated SDC shape verification prior to human airlock review.&#10;  - Zero external network egress: LLMs cannot communicate with external model servers or leak data outside the enclave.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 6. Automated Policy-as-Code Technical Architecture&#10;Governance is not maintained on paper; it is compiled into deterministic runtime enforcement code:&#10;1. **Semantic Ontology (&#92;&#96;ontology/*.ttl&#92;&#96;)**: Encodes Five Safes, hospital tiers, and Caldicott principles in OWL 2 RDF.&#10;2. **Closed-World Validation (&#92;&#96;ontology/shapes/*.ttl&#92;&#96;)**: Transforms the Open-World Assumption into deterministic Closed-World SHACL shapes.&#10;3. **Automated Compiler (&#92;&#96;compiler/rdf_to_rego.py&#92;&#96;)**: Compiles semantic shapes into Open Policy Agent (OPA) Rego rules.&#10;4. **Policy Enforcement Points (PEP)**: Kubernetes admission controllers intercept compute pod provisioning in microseconds.&#10;5. **Cryptographic Provenance**: Every admission and airlock decision emits a W3C PROV-O RDF triple to an append-only audit ledger.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 7. Public Transparency, Patient Opt-Out, and FOI Procedures&#10;- **National Data Opt-Out**: Respected in full. Patients who have opted out of secondary research have their records excluded prior to enclave ingestion.&#10;- **Freedom of Information (FOI)**: Under the FOI Act 2000, this Governance Charter and all compiled policy hashes are published openly.&#10;- **Audit Lineage Review**: Trust boards and external auditors receive automated monthly provenance digests generated via &#92;&#96;compiler/generate_docs.py&#92;&#96;.&#10;&#10;*Published by the Clinical Information Governance Board.* | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-166f6e999db425d33833-1-1 | [src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx](../src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx#L244) | 244 | # TRUSTED RESEARCH ENVIRONMENT (TRE) CLINICAL DATA GOVERNANCE CHARTER&#10;*Standard Operating Protocol &#38; Statutory Compliance Specification*&#10;*Document Reference: TRE-PAC-GOV-2026-V4 &#124; Approved by Trust Board &#38; Caldicott Guardian*&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 1. Statutory Mandate &#38; Legal Framework&#10;This platform operates in strict accordance with UK health data legislation and international standards:&#10;- **UK General Data Protection Regulation (UK GDPR)**:&#10;  - Article 6(1)(e): Processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 9(2)(j): Processing of special category health data is necessary for archiving and scientific research purposes in the public interest.&#10;- **Data Protection Act 2018 (DPA 2018)**: Part 2 and Section 19 safeguards for scientific research.&#10;- **National Health Service Act 2006 (Section 251)**: Statutory exemption granted on advice of the Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG).&#10;- **ISO/IEC 42001:2023**: Artificial Intelligence Management System (AIMS) governance.&#10;- **DCB0129 &#38; DCB0160**: NHS Clinical Risk Management for Health Software and Deployment.&#10;- **UK MDR 2002 &#38; MHRA SaMD**: Software as a Medical Device risk control and classification.&#10;- **Common Law Duty of Confidentiality (CLDC)**: Enforced via the Eight Caldicott Principles.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 2. Institutional Roles &#38; Oversight Structure&#10;- **Caldicott Guardian**: Responsible for championing confidentiality and reviewing all Tier 3 data access requests.&#10;- **Senior Information Risk Owner (SIRO)**: Board-level accountability for technical and cyber risks across the cloud enclave.&#10;- **Clinical Safety Officer (CSO)**: Registered clinician responsible for DCB0129 Hazard Logs and Safety Cases.&#10;- **Data Protection Officer (DPO)**: Conducts Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIA) and audits compliance with UK GDPR.&#10;- **Research Ethics Committee (REC)**: Independent health ethics body reviewing all research questions for demonstrable public benefit.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 3. The Seawater Tidal Model &#38; Five Safes&#10;- **Flood Tide (Ingress)**: Partitioned EHR and genomic data ingest through cryptographically audited pipelines.&#10;- **Safe Setting**: Airgapped digital safe room; no USB copying, no local downloads, zero outbound internet.&#10;- **Ebb Tide (Egress)**: Safe outputs pass through mathematical airlocks enforcing cell suppression ($k &#92;&#92;ge 10$) and dominance masking.&#10;&#10;*Published by the Clinical Information Governance Board.* | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-c6a4a13c5970a72f6769-1-1 | [src/components/PlaybookReader.tsx](../src/components/PlaybookReader.tsx#L50) | 50 | • The Seawater Maritime Problem Space &#38; Tidal Navigation: Clinical and genomic research operates not within a static quarantine pond, but across an open, turbulent maritime problem space dubbed "Seawater". Vessels—ranging from light exploratory query scripts to massive foundation model training clusters—must navigate shifting regulatory channels, unpredictable swells of adversarial prompt injections, and hidden underwater reefs of de-anonymization. The tide flows cyclically: the incoming flood tide brings massive surges of EHR, multi-omics, imaging, and registry microdata (ingress), while the receding ebb tide carries out derived statistical aggregates, trained model weights, and clinical discoveries (egress). Without active harbor defenses, salinity gatekeepers (W3C SHACL shape validation), and algorithmic sea-locks (microsecond OPA Rego policies), the enclave suffers either stagnation behind impassable bureaucratic drydocks or catastrophic contamination and leakage out into the open ocean. | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

The Classic Infrastructure Trap: Traditional IT configurations mistakenly hardcode governance rules directly into cluster network rules or pod name whitelists (e.g. "allow IP 10.244.2.14 to access clinical database X"). | C-ef8eed4917fa9e509aeb-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L422) | 422 | # Common SDC Rules &#38; Analytical Applications Matrix:&#10;1. Primary Cell Suppression: k &#62;= 10 (or k &#62;= 5). Any count between 1 and k-1 is suppressed.&#10;2. Dominance / (n, k) Rule: Top 1 contributor &#60;= 50%, top 2 contributors &#60;= 80% of aggregate sum.&#10;3. Quasi-Identifiers: Dates of birth, admission/discharge timestamps, partial postcodes, and rare diagnoses form high-dimensional fingerprints that re-identify individuals even without names.&#10;4. Logistic Regression: Suppress extreme odds ratios and parameter estimates where contingency cells are sparse (preventing quasi-complete separation disclosure).&#10;5. Survival Analysis (Kaplan-Meier): Smooth or truncate survival curves when the active risk set falls below 10; suppress individual tick marks for censored patients.&#10;6. Continuous Statistics: Suppress true minimums and maximums; replace with 5th and 95th percentiles. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
| C-206532571d6d9987a31a-10-3 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L429) | 429 | primary-suppression | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
| C-92a09bcf7ca97dd8cfef-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L436) | 436 | Indirect identifying variables (including appointment timestamps, hospital admission/discharge dates, birth year/month, partial postcode districts e.g. SW1A, sex, ethnicity, and rare clinical diagnosis codes). Sweeney’s landmark theorem demonstrated that 87% of the US/UK population is uniquely identifiable by just 5-digit ZIP code/postcode, gender, and date of birth. In healthcare research, removing patient names does not confer true anonymity: linking multiple quasi-identifiers against voter registries, local news, or obituary notices can immediately re-identify patients. Under UK GDPR and Caldicott Principle 1, such row-level datasets remain confidential personal data requiring strict airlock suppression, k-anonymity grouping, and AST scrubbing. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-74cbbbcf290f5b8f0356-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L715) | 715 | Clinical and genomic research takes place not in static isolated ponds, but in an open, turbulent maritime problem space dubbed "Seawater". Vessels—ranging from light exploratory scripts to heavy multi-node foundation model training runs—must navigate through shifting regulatory channels, unpredictable swells of adversarial prompt injections, and deep underwater hazards of de-anonymization. The tide flows cyclically: the flood tide brings in massive volumes of EHR, genomics, and registry microdata (ingress), while the receding ebb tide carries out derived statistical aggregates, trained model weights, and research publications (egress). Without active harbor defenses, salinity gatekeepers (W3C SHACL shape validation), and algorithmic sea-locks (microsecond OPA Rego policies), the enclave suffers either stagnation behind impassable bureaucratic drydocks or catastrophic contamination and leakage out into the open ocean. | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
| C-e671eb5e56f02c0ad8ed-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L715) | 715 | Clinical and genomic research takes place within an active, continuous data problem space dubbed "Seawater". Analytics workloads (ranging from exploratory query scripts to distributed foundation model pipelines) must navigate regulatory requirements, clinical governance channels, and patient confidentiality thresholds. The tide flows cyclically across two managed boundaries: Ingress and Egress. Ingress enforces gateway access controls, researcher accreditation, institutional identity verification, and Information Governance training (Safe People), alongside approved REC ethics protocols and Section 251 CAG exemptions (Safe Projects). Inbound clinical datasets, multi-omics records, and container workloads are verified at the boundary using W3C SHACL shape validation before admission. Egress regulates the export of derived analytical findings, statistical summaries, and model parameters, requiring automated Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) checks, k-anonymity (k &#62;= 10), and sub-millisecond Open Policy Agent (OPA) Rego policy evaluations to prevent patient re-identification. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
